\left\langle\hskip-3.01385pt\left\langle{P},{\mathcal{L}_{Z}}\right\rangle\hskip-3.01385pt\right\rangle=1

if and only if it starts at a vertex
u_{j}
(with
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
) and ends at a vertex
v_{k}
(with
k\in\{1,\ldots,d-1\}
) without visiting any other external vertex.
For
j\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\Delta(u_{j},\ell)
be the set of all such paths
P\in Z_{\mathrm{ext}}(\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{dec}^{*}})
that start at
u_{j}
and end at some vertex in the set
\{v_{k}\}_{k=1}^{d-1}
.
By the same argument as used for showing (
152
), we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}_{Z}}(p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\sum_{\ell=L^{\min}_{j}}^{L^{\max}_{j}}\binom{\ell}{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}\cdot|\Delta(u_{j},\ell)|\cdot p^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}\ .

(448)

Here
L^{\min}_{j}
and
L^{\max}_{j}
are the minimal and maximal lengths of a path
P\in Z_{\mathrm{ext}}(\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{dec}^{*}})
starting at
u_{j}
and ending in
\{v_{k}\}_{k=1}^{d-1}
.

The set
\Delta(u_{1},1)
is empty and the set
\Delta(u_{1},2)
consists of a single path, i.e.,
|\Delta(u_{1},1)|=0
and
\absolutevalue{\Delta(u_1, 2)}=1
. For
j\in\{2,\ldots,d-1\}
, we use that the graph
\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
has vertices of degree at most
4
, which implies that

\displaystyle|\Delta(u_{j},\ell)|\leq 3^{\ell-1}\qquad\textrm{ for all }\qquad\ell\in\mathbb{N}\

(449)

as in the proof of Lemma
5.5
.
Again using

\displaystyle L_{j}^{\min}\geq j\ .

(450)

and the inequalities
p^{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}\leq p^{\ell/2}
and
\binom{\ell}{\lceil\ell/2\rceil}\leq 2^{\ell}
for
\ell>1
in the expression (
448
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}_{Z}^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq\binom{2}{1}1\cdot p+\sum_{\ell=3}^{L_{1}^{\max}}2^{\ell}3^{\ell-1}p^{\ell/2}+\sum_{j=2}^{d}\sum_{\ell=j}^{L_{j}^{\max}}2^{\ell}3^{\ell-1}p^{\ell/2}

(451)

\displaystyle\leq 2p+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{\ell=3}^{\infty}q^{\ell}+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{j=2}^{d}\sum_{\ell=j}^{\infty}q^{\ell}\qquad\textrm{ where }\qquad q:=6\sqrt{p}

(452)

\displaystyle=2p+\frac{q^{3}}{3(1-q)}+\frac{q^{2}-q^{d-1}}{3(1-q)^{2}}.

(453)

Again,
q
satisfies
q\leq 6\sqrt{\frac{1}{144}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
For such
q
, the inequalities
\frac{q}{(1-q)}\leq 1
and
\frac{1}{(1-q)^{2}}\leq 4
hold and we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}_{Z}^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq 2p+\frac{1}{3}\cdot(6\sqrt{p})^{2}+\frac{4}{3}\cdot(6\sqrt{p})^{2}

(454)

\displaystyle=38p\ .

(455)

∎

Appendix C
An upper bound on the resilience function
\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathrm{cl},Z}}(p)

In this appendix,
we derive an upper bound on the resilience function
\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathrm{cl},Z}}(p)
. That is, we give the

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
7.1
. As before, it is sufficient to consider the set
Z_{\mathrm{ext}}(\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}})
in order to compute the resilience
\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathrm{cl},Z}}(p)
.
We partition the set of external vertices

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}=\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}\cup\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{top}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}

(456)

into the set
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
of vertices
(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})
forming the “bottom” boundary with
u_{2}=-1
and the set
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{top}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
remaining vertices which form the boundaries on the “top” (
u_{2}=r
), “front” (
u_{1}=1
) and “back” (
u_{1}=R
).
We label the external vertices as

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\left\{v_{i}^{k}\mid 1\leq i\leq d-1,k\in\{1,D\}\right\}\cup\left\{v_{i}^{k}\mid 0\leq i\leq d-1,2\leq k\leq\frac{R-1}{2}\right\}

(457)

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{top}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}

\displaystyle=\left\{u_{i,j}^{k}\mid 1\leq i\leq j,1\leq j\leq d-1,k\in\{1,\frac{R-1}{2}\}\right\}

(458)

\displaystyle\quad\cup\left\{u_{i}^{k}\mid 1\leq i\leq d,2\leq k\leq\frac{R-1}{2}\right\}\cup\left\{u_{d}^{1},u_{d}^{\frac{R-1}{2}}\right\}\ ,

(459)

see Fig.
10
.
Written out, the external vertices are located at the following (dual) sites:

\displaystyle v_{i}^{k}

\displaystyle=(2i+1,-1,2k-1)

(460)

\displaystyle u_{i,j}^{k}

\displaystyle=(2i-1,2j-1,2k-1)

(461)

\displaystyle u_{i}^{k}

\displaystyle=(2i-1,2d-1,2k-1)\ .

(462)

We introduce some subsets of paths in
Z_{\mathrm{ext}}(\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}})
.
Let us denote by
\Delta(v,\ell)
the set of all simple paths through internal vertices starting at
v\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
and ending at any vertex in
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{top}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
.
Then, by the similar argument as for showing (
317
), we have

\displaystyle\left\{P\in Z_{\mathrm{ext}}(\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}})\mid\absolutevalue{P}=\ell\textrm{ and }\left\langle\hskip-3.01385pt\left\langle{P},{\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathrm{cl},Z}}\right\rangle\hskip-3.01385pt\right\rangle=1\right\}=\bigcup_{v\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}}\Delta(v,\ell)\qquad\textrm{ for }\qquad\ell\geq 1\ ,

(463)

and the subsets of paths
\left\{\Delta(v,\ell)\right\}_{v\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}},\ell\geq 1}
are pairwise disjoint.
Let
L_{v}^{\min}
and
L_{v}^{\max}
be the minimal and maximal lengths of paths in
\bigcup_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\Delta(v,\ell)
for
v\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
,
and let us define

\displaystyle\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}(s)=\left\{v\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}\mid L_{v}^{\min}=s\right\}\ .

(464)

It is easy to check from the definition of
\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
that for each vertex
v=(v_{1},v_{2},v_{3})\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
there exists a path of length at most
d
which starts from
v
and ends at some
u\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{top}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
with
(v_{1},v_{3})=(u_{1},u_{3})
, and this guarantees that the set
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}(s)
is empty for all
s>d
.
Moreover, we observe that
\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}(1)
is empty.
With an analogous argument as in the proof of Lemma
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathrm{cl},Z}}(p)=\sum_{s=2}^{d}B_{s}\qquad\textrm{ where }\qquad B_{s}=\sum_{v\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}(s)}\sum_{\ell=s}^{L_{v}^{\max}}\binom{\ell}{\lceil{\ell/2}\rceil}\cdot\absolutevalue{\Delta(v, \ell)}\cdot p^{\lceil{\ell/2}\rceil}\ .

(465)

Here we note that each vertex in the graph
\mathsf{T}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}
has degree at most
6
, and this implies similarly as before that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Delta(u,\ell)}\leq 5^{\ell}\qquad\textrm{ for }\qquad u\in\mathsf{V}^{\mathrm{ext},\mathrm{bottom}}_{\mathrm{cl},\mathrm{dec}^{*}}\ .

(466)

We first calculate an upper bound on
B_{d}
.
Due to the bound

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Vextbottom{d} }\leq\absolutevalue{\Vcldecdualextbottom}\leq d\cdot\frac{R+1}{2}

(467)

on the number of external vertices on the bottom, together with the bound (
466
) and the fact that
\binom{\ell}{\lceil{\ell/2}\rceil}\leq 2^{l}
and
p^{\lceil{\ell/2}\rceil}\leq p^{\ell/2}
, we have

\displaystyle B_{d}

\displaystyle\leq d\cdot\frac{R+1}{2}\sum_{\ell=d}^{\infty}2^{\ell}\cdot 5^{\ell}\cdot p^{\ell/2}

(468)

\displaystyle\leq d\cdot\frac{R+1}{2}\cdot\frac{q^{d}}{1-q}\qquad\textrm{ where }\qquad q:=10\sqrt{p}\ .

(469)

Here the geometric series converges since
0\leq q=10\sqrt{p}\leq\frac{1}{2}

It remains to calculate an upper bound of
\sum_{s=2}^{d-1}B_{s}
. It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\Vextbottom{s}}\leq 2\cdot s\qquad\textrm{ for }\qquad 2\leq s\leq d-1\ .

(470)

By analogous reasoning as for showing (
469
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=2}^{d-1}B_{s}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=2}^{d-1}\left(2\cdot s\sum_{\ell=s}^{\infty}2^{\ell}\cdot 5^{\ell}\cdot p^{\ell/2}\right)

(471)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=2}^{d-1}2\cdot s\cdot\frac{q^{s}}{1-q}

(472)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4q^{2}}{\left(1-q\right)^{2}}+\frac{2q^{3}}{\left(1-q\right)^{3}}\ .

(473)

Again, the geometric series and the arithmetico-geometric series converge since
0\leq q\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Inserting
q=10\sqrt{p}
, (
469
) and (
473
) into (
465
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{res}_{\mathcal{L}^{*}_{\mathrm{cl},Z}}(p)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4q^{2}}{(1-q)^{2}}+\frac{2q^{3}}{(1-q)^{3}}+d\cdot\frac{R+1}{2}\cdot\frac{q^{d}}{1-q}

(474)

\displaystyle\leq\left[\frac{400}{(1-q)^{2}}+\frac{200q}{(1-q)^{3}}+50d(R+1)\cdot\frac{(10\sqrt{p})^{d-2}}{1-q}\right]\cdot p.

(475)

The bound (
339
) follows from (
475
) because
q\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
\frac{1}{1-q}\leq 2
.
∎
